## Complete Agent Implementation Analysis

I've thoroughly explored the agent implementation in this Go project. Here's the complete picture of how data flows from release ingestion to semantic release generation:

### 1. AGENT PROMPT / INSTRUCTIONS

**Location**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go:38-57`

**Default Instruction** (can be overridden per-project):
```go
const DefaultInstruction = `You are a release intelligence agent for a software project.
Your job is to analyze recent upstream releases from the project's tracked sources
and produce a structured semantic release report.

Use the available tools to:
1. Fetch the list of recent releases for this project.
2. Inspect individual release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads).
3. Review the project's context sources (runbooks, documentation) for background.

CRITICAL: Your final response MUST be a single JSON object and nothing else.
Do not include any explanation, commentary, or markdown formatting — just the raw JSON.

The JSON object must have exactly these fields:
{
  "summary": "A 2-3 sentence high-level summary of what changed across the releases.",
  "availability": "Are these releases available and stable? (e.g., 'GA', 'RC', 'Beta')",
  "adoption": "What is the recommended adoption timeline? (e.g., 'Immediate', 'Next sprint', 'Monitor')",
  "urgency": "How urgent is upgrading? (e.g., 'Critical', 'High', 'Medium', 'Low')",
  "recommendation": "A concrete 1-2 sentence recommendation for the team."
}`
```

**Custom Prompt**: Each `Project` model can have a custom `AgentPrompt` field; if set, it's prepended to the default instruction (line 66):
```go
instruction := DefaultInstruction
if project.AgentPrompt != "" {
	instruction = project.AgentPrompt + "\n\n" + instruction
}
```

---

### 2. THE ORCHESTRATOR

**Location**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go:94-307`

**Key Component**: `Orchestrator` struct manages the full agent lifecycle.

**Core Method**: `RunAgent(ctx, run *models.AgentRun) error` (lines 127-157)

**Execution Flow**:
1. **Marks run as "running"** — Updates DB status
2. **Calls `executeAgent()`** — Does the actual work
3. **On success**: Creates semantic release, updates run result, marks as "completed"
4. **On failure**: Marks run as "failed", logs error
5. **Finally**: Sends project-level notifications (best-effort)

**executeAgent() Workflow** (lines 170-307):
1. **Load project** from DB
2. **Build agent** using `BuildAgent()` helper (line 182)
3. **Create ADK session** — In-memory session service from ADK-Go
4. **Create runner** — ADK runner wraps the agent
5. **Run the agent** with user prompt: `"Analyze the recent releases for this project and produce a semantic release report."`
6. **Extract final text** from agent events
7. **Parse response** as JSON using `parseReport()` — Falls back to raw text if invalid JSON
8. **Gather release IDs** — Lists recent releases to link semantic release to source releases
9. **Create SemanticRelease** — Persists to DB with JSON report

**Data Passed to Agent**:
- Via `BuildAgent()`: LLM model, ADK tools, project-specific instruction (with custom prompt if configured)
- Via tools during execution: Available releases, context sources

---

### 3. THE AGENT TOOLS

**Location**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/tools.go`

**Three ADK Function Tools Created** (lines 94-122):

#### Tool 1: `get_releases`
- **Description**: Fetch recent releases for the current project
- **Input**: `GetReleasesInput` (optional page, per_page)
- **Output**: `GetReleasesOutput` — paginated releases with version, raw_data, timestamps
- **Handler**: `f.getReleases()` (lines 125-161)
- **Data Source**: `AgentDataStore.ListReleasesByProject()`

#### Tool 2: `get_release_detail`
- **Description**: Get detailed information for a specific release by ID
- **Input**: `GetReleaseDetailInput` (release_id required)
- **Output**: `GetReleaseDetailOutput` — full release with raw_data payload
- **Handler**: `f.getReleaseDetail()` (lines 164-186)
- **Data Source**: `AgentDataStore.GetRelease()`

#### Tool 3: `list_context_sources`
- **Description**: List project's context sources (runbooks, docs, dashboards)
- **Input**: `ListContextSourcesInput` (optional page, per_page)
- **Output**: `ListContextSourcesOutput` — paginated context sources with type, name, config
- **Handler**: `f.listContextSources()` (lines 189-220)
- **Data Source**: `AgentDataStore.ListContextSources()`

**Tool Factory**: All tools are bound to a specific `projectID` — data queries are scoped per project.

**Data Transformation**: Tools convert DB models to JSON-serializable structures with ISO8601 timestamps.

---

### 4. THE AGENT WORKER

**Location**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/worker.go`

**Component**: `AgentWorker` implements `river.WorkerDefaults[queue.AgentJobArgs]`

**Job Type**: `AgentJobArgs` (from `/internal/queue/jobs.go:18-21`)
```go
type AgentJobArgs struct {
	AgentRunID string `json:"agent_run_id"`
	ProjectID  string `json:"project_id"`
}
```

**Worker Lifecycle** - `Work()` method (lines 32-72):
1. **Log job pickup** with attempt number
2. **Load agent run** from DB using `AgentRunID`
3. **Validate** run exists, extract project ID and trigger info
4. **Delegate to orchestrator** — `w.orchestrator.RunAgent(ctx, run)`
5. **On success**: Return nil (job completes)
6. **On error**: Log and return error (River retries based on config)

**River Integration**: River client handles job retrieval, retry logic, and completion tracking.

---

### 5. HOW RELEASES ARE FETCHED AND PASSED TO AGENT

**Release Ingestion Flow** (Upstream to Agent):
1. **Polling Workers** (IIngestionSource) — Poll upstream registries (Docker Hub, GitHub, etc.)
2. **Return IngestionResult** — Raw version, changelog, metadata, timestamp
3. **Service.ProcessResults()** — Persists each result via store
4. **IngestRelease() Transaction** (in `/internal/ingestion/pgstore.go:26-59`):
   - Insert release row with (source_id, version, raw_data, released_at)
   - Enqueue NotifyJobArgs in **same transaction** (transactional outbox pattern)
   - Commit both together (zero-loss guarantee)

**From Release Storage to Agent**:
1. **NotifyWorker processes NotifyJobArgs** — Sends source-level notifications
2. **Checks AgentRules** (lines 88-143 in routing/worker.go) against version:
   - Pattern match logic (on_major_release, on_minor_release, on_security_patch, version_pattern)
   - If match: Enqueue AgentJobArgs
3. **AgentWorker picks up AgentJobArgs**
4. **Calls Orchestrator.RunAgent()**
5. **BuildAgent() → uses AgentDataStore to query recent releases**:
   - `ListReleasesByProject()` — Gets ordered list of recent releases
   - Tools expose these to the LLM agent

**What the Agent Sees**:
- Paginated `ReleaseItem` objects with:
  - `id`, `source_id`, `version` (identifiers)
  - `raw_data` (full JSON from upstream provider)
  - `released_at`, `created_at` (timestamps)
- Context source metadata (documentation, runbooks)

---

### 6. ADK-GO INTEGRATION

**Location**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/model.go` + imports from `google.golang.org/adk`

**ADK-Go Version**: `v0.5.0` (from go.mod)

**LLM Model Creation** (lines 28-47):
```go
func NewLLMModel(ctx context.Context, cfg LLMConfig) (model.LLM, error) {
	switch cfg.Provider {
	case "gemini", "":
		// Uses google.golang.org/adk/model/gemini
		return gemini.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, &genai.ClientConfig{APIKey: cfg.GoogleAPIKey})
	case "openai":
		// Custom OpenAI implementation at internal/agent/openai/openai.go
		return oaimodel.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, oaimodel.Config{APIKey: cfg.OpenAIAPIKey, BaseURL: cfg.OpenAIBaseURL})
	}
}
```

**Agent Creation** - `BuildAgent()` (lines 62-89):
```go
return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
	Name:        "release_analyst",
	Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
	Model:       llmModel,
	Instruction: instruction,  // Project-specific prompt
	Tools:       tools,         // 3 agent tools (get_releases, get_release_detail, list_context_sources)
})
```

**Runner & Session** (lines 188-206):
- Creates in-memory ADK session service
- Creates ADK runner wrapping the agent
- Runs agent with user message

**Event Stream Processing** (lines 217-234):
- Iterates through ADK events from `r.Run()`
- Captures `IsFinalResponse()` event
- Extracts text from `event.Content.Parts`
- Accumulates `finalText`

**OpenAI Integration** (lines 38-44 & `/internal/agent/openai/openai.go`):
- Custom `openaiModel` implements `model.LLM` interface
- Converts ADK `LLMRequest` → OpenAI chat completion request
- Converts OpenAI response → ADK `LLMResponse`
- Handles tool calls and function responses
- Supports configurable BaseURL (for compatibility APIs)

---

### 7. DATA MODELS

**Release** (`/internal/models/release.go`):
```go
type Release struct {
	ID         string            // UUID
	SourceID   string            // Foreign key to sources
	Version    string            // e.g., "v1.2.3"
	RawData    json.RawMessage   // Full upstream provider data
	ReleasedAt *time.Time        // Optional release timestamp from upstream
	CreatedAt  time.Time         // When ingested
}
```

**Project** (`/internal/models/project.go`):
```go
type Project struct {
	ID          string            // UUID
	Name        string
	Description string
	AgentPrompt string            // Custom prompt prepended to default
	AgentRules  json.RawMessage   // Version matching rules (OnMajor, OnMinor, OnSecurityPatch, VersionPattern)
	CreatedAt   time.Time
	UpdatedAt   time.Time
}
```

**AgentRun** (`/internal/models/agent_run.go`):
```go
type AgentRun struct {
	ID                string      // UUID
	ProjectID         string      // FK to projects
	SemanticReleaseID *string     // FK to semantic_releases (set on completion)
	Trigger           string      // e.g., "auto:version:v1.2.3" or manual trigger info
	Status            string      // "pending" → "running" → "completed" or "failed"
	PromptUsed        string      // (unused in current code)
	Error             string      // Error message if failed
	StartedAt         *time.Time  // When transitioned to running
	CompletedAt       *time.Time  // When completed
	CreatedAt         time.Time
}
```

**SemanticRelease** (`/internal/models/semantic_release.go`):
```go
type SemanticRelease struct {
	ID          string            // UUID
	ProjectID   string            // FK to projects
	Version     string            // e.g., "v1.2.3" (copied from latest release)
	Report      json.RawMessage   // Parsed SemanticReport JSON
	Status      string            // "completed" or "failed"
	Error       string            // Error if failed
	CreatedAt   time.Time
	CompletedAt *time.Time
}

type SemanticReport struct {
	Summary        string  // 2-3 sentence overview
	Availability   string  // GA, RC, Beta, etc.
	Adoption       string  // Immediate, Next sprint, Monitor
	Urgency        string  // Critical, High, Medium, Low
	Recommendation string  // Actionable recommendation
}
```

**ContextSource** (`/internal/models/context_source.go`):
```go
type ContextSource struct {
	ID        string            // UUID
	ProjectID string            // FK to projects
	Type      string            // e.g., "runbook", "documentation", "dashboard"
	Name      string
	Config    json.RawMessage   // Type-specific config (URL, credentials, etc.)
	CreatedAt time.Time
	UpdatedAt time.Time
}
```

---

### 8. COMPLETE DATA FLOW DIAGRAM

```
IIngestionSource (Docker Hub, GitHub, etc.)
    ↓ FetchNewReleases()
IngestionResult (version, changelog, metadata)
    ↓ Service.ProcessResults()
    ↓ Store.IngestRelease() [TRANSACTION]
        ├─ INSERT releases (id, source_id, version, raw_data, released_at)
        └─ INSERT river job: NotifyJobArgs[release_id, source_id]
    ↓
NotifyWorker.Work()
    ├─ Sends source-level notifications
    └─ CheckAgentRules() — Version pattern matching
        ↓ If rules match
        ├─ Create agent_run (status=pending, trigger=auto:version:X)
        └─ INSERT river job: AgentJobArgs[agent_run_id, project_id]
    ↓
AgentWorker.Work()
    ↓ Load AgentRun
    ↓ Orchestrator.RunAgent()
        ├─ Update status → "running"
        ├─ Load Project (with optional AgentPrompt)
        ├─ BuildAgent()
        │   ├─ Create LLM model (Gemini or OpenAI)
        │   └─ Create 3 tools (get_releases, get_release_detail, list_context_sources)
        ├─ ADK-Go Session & Runner
        ├─ Run agent with user prompt "Analyze recent releases..."
        │   ├─ Agent calls get_releases() → Lists recent source releases
        │   ├─ Agent calls get_release_detail() → Gets full upstream data
        │   ├─ Agent calls list_context_sources() → Gets runbooks, docs
        │   └─ LLM generates semantic analysis
        ├─ Parse finalText as JSON (SemanticReport)
        ├─ Create SemanticRelease with JSON report
        ├─ Link to source releases (semantic_release_sources join table)
        ├─ Update status → "completed"
        └─ Send project-level notifications (Slack, Discord, Webhook)
    ↓
SemanticRelease stored in DB with AI-generated analysis
```

---

### 9. KEY DESIGN PATTERNS

**Transactional Outbox**: Release insert + River job enqueue happen in one transaction (zero-loss guarantee)

**Project-Scoped Tools**: All agent tools bound to specific project ID — agent can't see releases from other projects

**Version-Based Triggering**: Agent runs triggered by semantic versioning rules (major, minor, security patch)

**Custom Prompts**: Per-project `AgentPrompt` can be set to customize analysis behavior

**Graceful Degradation**: If LLM fails to produce valid JSON, raw text is stored as summary

**Best-Effort Notifications**: Project-level notifications are sent after agent completes but errors are logged, not fatal

---

**Summary**: The agent receives structured release data through three tools, uses ADK-Go to orchestrate LLM calls (Gemini or OpenAI), analyzes recent releases with optional project-specific context, and produces a typed semantic release report that's persisted and optionally broadcast to notification channels.

## Research Summary: ADK-Go Built-in Tools and Google Search

Based on my research of the ReleaseBeacon codebase and ADK-Go documentation, here are the key findings:

### 1. **ADK-Go Version and Dependencies**

Your project uses **ADK-Go v0.5.0** (declared in `go.mod`):
```go
require (
    google.golang.org/adk v0.5.0
    google.golang.org/genai v1.40.0
)
```

### 2. **Current Tool Usage Pattern in Your Codebase**

In `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/tools.go`, tools are created using the `functiontool` package:

```go
import (
    "google.golang.org/adk/tool"
    "google.golang.org/adk/tool/functiontool"
)

// Tools are created like this:
getReleases, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
    Name:        "get_releases",
    Description: "...",
}, f.getReleases)
```

The agent is built in `orchestrator.go` using `llmagent.New()` with the tools array.

### 3. **Google Search Built-in Tool**

**Google Search is a Gemini-native tool**, not a standard function tool. It's accessed through:

**Import Path:**
```go
import "google.golang.org/adk/tool/geminitool"
```

**Basic Usage:**
```go
tools := []tool.Tool{
    geminitool.GoogleSearch{},  // Simple struct, no initialization needed
}

agent := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    Name:        "my_agent",
    Model:       model,
    Tools:       tools,
    Instruction: "Use Google Search when you need current information...",
})
```

### 4. **GoogleSearch Struct and Methods**

The `GoogleSearch` type is a **zero-value struct** with four interface methods:

| Method | Signature | Returns |
|--------|-----------|---------|
| `Name()` | `func (s GoogleSearch) Name() string` | `"google_search"` |
| `Description()` | `func (s GoogleSearch) Description() string` | "Performs a Google search to retrieve information from the web." |
| `ProcessRequest()` | `func (s GoogleSearch) ProcessRequest(ctx tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error` | Injects `&genai.Tool{GoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{}}` into the LLM request |
| `IsLongRunning()` | `func (s GoogleSearch) IsLongRunning() bool` | `false` |

### 5. **Important Constraint: Multiple Tools Workaround**

Google Search as a **grounding tool** has constraints when combined with **custom function tools** in a single agent. The ADK-Go repository includes an example workaround:

**Pattern:** Use **sub-agents** with `agenttool.New()`:

```go
import (
    "google.golang.org/adk/tool/agenttool"
    "google.golang.org/adk/tool/functiontool"
    "google.golang.org/adk/tool/geminitool"
    "google.golang.org/adk/agent/llmagent"
)

// Sub-agent 1: Google Search
searchAgent := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    Name: "search_agent",
    Tools: []tool.Tool{geminitool.GoogleSearch{}},
})

// Sub-agent 2: Custom functions
customAgent := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    Name: "custom_agent",
    Tools: []tool.Tool{customFunctionTool},
})

// Root agent: combines both via agenttool
rootAgent := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    Name: "root_agent",
    Tools: []tool.Tool{
        agenttool.New("search", searchAgent),
        agenttool.New("custom", customAgent),
    },
    Instruction: "Delegate to appropriate sub-agent...",
})
```

**Real Example:** See [google/adk-go/examples/tools/multipletools](https://github.com/google/adk-go/blob/v0.5.0/examples/tools/multipletools/main.go)

### 6. **How Google Search Works**

- Google Search is **automatically invoked by Gemini 2 models** when the model determines real-time info is needed
- It **operates server-side** within the Gemini model — no local code execution
- Responses include `groundingMetadata` with source web pages and citations
- No additional permissions or API keys required beyond `GOOGLE_API_KEY` (already in your config)

### 7. **Configuration for Your Codebase**

To add Google Search to ReleaseBeacon's agent, you would need to either:

**Option A (Simplest - breaking constraint):** Add it directly if your model and use case don't trigger the constraint:
```go
import "google.golang.org/adk/tool/geminitool"

tools, err := NewTools(store, project.ID)  // Your custom tools
tools = append(tools, geminitool.GoogleSearch{})
```

**Option B (Safer - using sub-agents):** Refactor to use sub-agents as shown above to avoid the tool-mixing constraint.

### Sources

- [Google ADK-Go GitHub Repository](https://github.com/google/adk-go)
- [ADK-Go Package Documentation (pkg.go.dev)](https://pkg.go.dev/google.golang.org/adk)
- [geminitool Package Reference](https://pkg.go.dev/google.golang.org/adk/tool/geminitool)

    24→// OrchestratorStore defines all data access methods required by the agent
    25→// orchestrator to load project configuration, run the agent, and persist
    26→// the resulting semantic release.
    27→type OrchestratorStore interface {
    28→	AgentDataStore
    29→	GetProject(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Project, error)
    30→	GetAgentRun(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.AgentRun, error)
    31→	UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx context.Context, id, status string) error
    32→	CreateSemanticRelease(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SemanticRelease, releaseIDs []string) error
    33→	UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx context.Context, id string, semanticReleaseID string) error
    34→	ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx context.Context, projectID string) ([]models.Subscription, error)
    35→	GetChannel(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.NotificationChannel, error)
    36→}
    37→
    38→const DefaultInstruction = `You are a release intelligence agent for a software project.
    39→Your job is to analyze recent upstream releases from the project's tracked sources
    40→and produce a structured semantic release report.
    41→
    42→Use the available tools to:
    43→1. Fetch the list of recent releases for this project.
    44→2. Inspect individual release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads).
    45→3. Review the project's context sources (runbooks, documentation) for background.
    46→
    47→CRITICAL: Your final response MUST be a single JSON object and nothing else.
    48→Do not include any explanation, commentary, or markdown formatting — just the raw JSON.
    49→
    50→The JSON object must have exactly these fields:
    51→{
    52→  "summary": "A 2-3 sentence high-level summary of what changed across the releases.",
    53→  "availability": "Are these releases available and stable? (e.g., 'GA', 'RC', 'Beta')",
    54→  "adoption": "What is the recommended adoption timeline? (e.g., 'Immediate', 'Next sprint', 'Monitor')",
    55→  "urgency": "How urgent is upgrading? (e.g., 'Critical', 'High', 'Medium', 'Low')",
    56→  "recommendation": "A concrete 1-2 sentence recommendation for the team."
    57→}`
    58→
    59→// BuildAgent creates the ADK-Go LLM agent for a given project. This is used
    60→// by both the production orchestrator and the dev entrypoint to ensure the
    61→// same agent configuration is used everywhere.
    62→func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig) (agent.Agent, error) {
    63→	// Build instruction from project's agent_prompt or use default.
    64→	instruction := DefaultInstruction
    65→	if project.AgentPrompt != "" {
    66→		instruction = project.AgentPrompt + "\n\n" + instruction
    67→	}
    68→
    69→	// Create LLM model.
    70→	llmModel, err := NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig)
    71→	if err != nil {
    72→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create LLM model: %w", err)
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	// Create project-scoped tools.
    76→	tools, err := NewTools(store, project.ID)
    77→	if err != nil {
    78→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create agent tools: %w", err)
    79→	}
    80→
    81→	// Create agent.
    82→	return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
    83→		Name:        "release_analyst",
    84→		Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
    85→		Model:       llmModel,
    86→		Instruction: instruction,
    87→		Tools:       tools,
    88→	})
    89→}
    90→
    91→// Orchestrator manages the lifecycle of an agent run: loading project config,
    92→// creating an ADK-Go agent with project-specific tools and instructions,
    93→// running the agent, and persisting the result as a semantic release.
    94→type Orchestrator struct {
    95→	store     OrchestratorStore
    96→	llmConfig LLMConfig
    97→}
    98→
    99→// NewOrchestrator creates a new Orchestrator. It validates that the provided
   100→// LLMConfig has the required API key for the configured provider. If the key
   101→// is missing, it returns an error so the caller can degrade gracefully.
   102→func NewOrchestrator(store OrchestratorStore, cfg LLMConfig) (*Orchestrator, error) {
   103→	switch cfg.Provider {
   104→	case "openai":
   105→		if cfg.OpenAIAPIKey == "" {
   106→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("OPENAI_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   107→		}
   108→	default: // gemini
   109→		if cfg.GoogleAPIKey == "" {
   110→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("GOOGLE_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   111→		}
   112→	}
   113→	return &Orchestrator{
   114→		store:     store,
   115→		llmConfig: cfg,
   116→	}, nil
   117→}
   118→
   119→// RunAgent executes the full agent lifecycle for a given agent run:
   120→//  1. Marks the run as "running".
   121→//  2. Loads the project and builds the agent prompt.
   122→//  3. Creates an ADK-Go LLM agent with project-scoped tools.
   123→//  4. Runs the agent and captures its final text response.
   124→//  5. Parses the response into a SemanticReport.
   125→//  6. Creates a SemanticRelease and links it to the agent run.
   126→//  7. Marks the run as "completed" (or "failed" on error).
   127→func (o *Orchestrator) RunAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) error {
   128→	// Mark as running.
   129→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "running"); err != nil {
   130→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to running: %w", err)
   131→	}
   132→
   133→	// Execute the agent; capture any error to mark the run as failed.
   134→	result, err := o.executeAgent(ctx, run)
   135→	if err != nil {
   136→		slog.Error("agent run failed", "run_id", run.ID, "err", err)
   137→		// Best-effort: mark the run as failed.
   138→		if statusErr := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "failed"); statusErr != nil {
   139→			slog.Error("agent: failed to mark run as failed",
   140→				"run_id", run.ID, "status_err", statusErr)
   141→		}
   142→		return err
   143→	}
   144→
   145→	// Link the semantic release to the agent run and mark completed.
   146→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx, run.ID, result.semanticReleaseID); err != nil {
   147→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run result: %w", err)
   148→	}
   149→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "completed"); err != nil {
   150→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to completed: %w", err)
   151→	}
   152→
   153→	// Send project-level notifications (best-effort; errors are logged, not returned).
   154→	o.sendProjectNotifications(ctx, run, result)
   155→
   156→	return nil
   157→}
   158→
   159→// agentResult holds the output of a successful agent execution, used to
   160→// construct notifications after the semantic release is persisted.
   161→type agentResult struct {
   162→	semanticReleaseID string
   163→	version           string
   164→	reportText        string
   165→	projectName       string
   166→}
   167→
   168→// executeAgent performs the actual LLM agent interaction and returns the
   169→// agentResult containing the semantic release ID and metadata for notifications.
   170→func (o *Orchestrator) executeAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) (*agentResult, error) {
   171→	// Load project.
   172→	slog.Info("agent: loading project", "run_id", run.ID, "project_id", run.ProjectID)
   173→	project, err := o.store.GetProject(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   174→	if err != nil {
   175→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("get project: %w", err)
   176→	}
   177→	slog.Info("agent: project loaded", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name)
   178→
   179→	// Build the agent using the shared constructor.
   180→	slog.Info("agent: building agent", "run_id", run.ID,
   181→		"provider", o.llmConfig.Provider, "model", o.llmConfig.Model)
   182→	agentInstance, err := BuildAgent(ctx, o.store, project, o.llmConfig)
   183→	if err != nil {
   184→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("build agent: %w", err)
   185→	}
   186→
   187→	// Create in-memory session service and a new session.
   188→	sessionService := session.InMemoryService()
   189→	createResp, err := sessionService.Create(ctx, &session.CreateRequest{
   190→		AppName: "changelogue",
   191→		UserID:  "system",
   192→	})
   193→	if err != nil {
   194→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create session: %w", err)
   195→	}
   196→	sess := createResp.Session
   197→
   198→	// Create the runner.
   199→	r, err := runner.New(runner.Config{
   200→		AppName:        "changelogue",
   201→		Agent:          agentInstance,
   202→		SessionService: sessionService,
   203→	})
   204→	if err != nil {
   205→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create runner: %w", err)
   206→	}
   207→
   208→	// Run the agent with a prompt requesting analysis.
   209→	slog.Info("agent: starting LLM run", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name)
   210→	userMsg := genai.NewContentFromText(
   211→		"Analyze the recent releases for this project and produce a semantic release report.",
   212→		"user",
   213→	)
   214→
   215→	var finalText string
   216→	eventCount := 0
   217→	for event, err := range r.Run(ctx, "system", sess.ID(), userMsg, agent.RunConfig{}) {
   218→		if err != nil {
   219→			slog.Error("agent: run event error",
   220→				"run_id", run.ID,
   221→				"event_count", eventCount,
   222→				"err", err,
   223→			)
   224→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent run event error: %w", err)
   225→		}
   226→		eventCount++
   227→		if event != nil && event.IsFinalResponse() && event.Content != nil {
   228→			for _, part := range event.Content.Parts {
   229→				if part.Text != "" {
   230→					finalText += part.Text
   231→				}
   232→			}
   233→		}
   234→	}
   235→	slog.Info("agent: LLM run finished",
   236→		"run_id", run.ID,
   237→		"event_count", eventCount,
   238→		"output_length", len(finalText),
   239→	)
   240→
   241→	if finalText == "" {
   242→		slog.Error("agent: produced no output",
   243→			"run_id", run.ID,
   244→			"project_id", run.ProjectID,
   245→			"event_count", eventCount,
   246→		)
   247→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent produced no output")
   248→	}
   249→
   250→	// Parse the agent's response into a SemanticReport.
   251→	report, err := parseReport(finalText)
   252→	if err != nil {
   253→		slog.Warn("agent output was not valid JSON report, storing raw",
   254→			"run_id", run.ID, "parse_err", err, "raw_output", finalText)
   255→		// Fall back to storing the raw text as the summary.
   256→		report = &models.SemanticReport{
   257→			Summary: finalText,
   258→		}
   259→	}
   260→
   261→	reportJSON, err := json.Marshal(report)
   262→	if err != nil {
   263→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("marshal report: %w", err)
   264→	}
   265→
   266→	// Gather release IDs for the semantic_release_sources join table.
   267→	releases, _, err := o.store.ListReleasesByProject(ctx, run.ProjectID, 1, 50)
   268→	if err != nil {
   269→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("list releases for semantic release: %w", err)
   270→	}
   271→	releaseIDs := make([]string, 0, len(releases))
   272→	for _, r := range releases {
   273→		releaseIDs = append(releaseIDs, r.ID)
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	// Determine a version label from the most recent release.
   277→	version := "unknown"
   278→	if len(releases) > 0 {
   279→		version = releases[0].Version
   280→	}
   281→
   282→	now := time.Now()
   283→	sr := &models.SemanticRelease{
   284→		ProjectID:   run.ProjectID,
   285→		Version:     version,
   286→		Report:      reportJSON,
   287→		Status:      "completed",
   288→		CompletedAt: &now,
   289→	}
   290→
   291→	if err := o.store.CreateSemanticRelease(ctx, sr, releaseIDs); err != nil {
   292→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create semantic release: %w", err)
   293→	}
   294→
   295→	slog.Info("agent run produced semantic release",
   296→		"run_id", run.ID,
   297→		"semantic_release_id", sr.ID,
   298→		"version", sr.Version,
   299→	)
   300→
   301→	return &agentResult{
   302→		semanticReleaseID: sr.ID,
   303→		version:           version,
   304→		reportText:        finalText,
   305→		projectName:       project.Name,
   306→	}, nil
   307→}
   308→
   309→// defaultSenders returns a map of channel type to Sender for the supported
   310→// notification channel types (webhook, slack, discord).
   311→func defaultSenders() map[string]routing.Sender {
   312→	httpClient := &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second}
   313→	return map[string]routing.Sender{
   314→		"webhook": &routing.WebhookSender{Client: httpClient},
   315→		"slack":   &routing.SlackSender{Client: httpClient},
   316→		"discord": &routing.DiscordSender{Client: httpClient},
   317→	}
   318→}
   319→
   320→// sendProjectNotifications looks up project-level subscriptions and sends a
   321→// notification to each channel with the semantic release report. This is
   322→// best-effort: errors are logged but do not propagate to the caller.
   323→func (o *Orchestrator) sendProjectNotifications(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun, result *agentResult) {
   324→	subs, err := o.store.ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   325→	if err != nil {
   326→		slog.Error("list project subscriptions for notification",
   327→			"project_id", run.ProjectID, "err", err)
   328→		return
   329→	}
   330→	if len(subs) == 0 {
   331→		slog.Debug("agent: no project subscriptions configured",
   332→			"project_id", run.ProjectID)
   333→		return
   334→	}
   335→
   336→	senders := defaultSenders()
   337→
   338→	msg := routing.Notification{
   339→		Title:   fmt.Sprintf("Semantic Release Report: %s %s", result.projectName, result.version),
   340→		Body:    result.reportText,
   341→		Version: result.version,
   342→	}
   343→
   344→	for _, sub := range subs {
   345→		ch, err := o.store.GetChannel(ctx, sub.ChannelID)
   346→		if err != nil {
   347→			slog.Error("get channel for project notification",
   348→				"channel_id", sub.ChannelID, "err", err)
   349→			continue
   350→		}
   351→
   352→		sender, ok := senders[ch.Type]
   353→		if !ok {
   354→			slog.Warn("unknown channel type for project notification", "type", ch.Type)
   355→			continue
   356→		}
   357→
   358→		if err := sender.Send(ctx, ch, msg); err != nil {
   359→			slog.Error("send project notification failed",
   360→				"channel", ch.Name, "type", ch.Type, "err", err)
   361→		} else {
   362→			slog.Info("project notification sent",
   363→				"channel", ch.Name, "project_id", run.ProjectID, "version", result.version)
   364→		}
   365→	}
   366→}
   367→
   368→// parseReport attempts to parse the agent's text output as a SemanticReport JSON.
   369→// It handles cases where the agent wraps the JSON in markdown code blocks.
   370→func parseReport(text string) (*models.SemanticReport, error) {
   371→	// Strip markdown code fences if present.
   372→	cleaned := strings.TrimSpace(text)
   373→	if strings.HasPrefix(cleaned, "```") {
   374→		// Remove opening fence (with optional language tag).
   375→		if idx := strings.Index(cleaned, "\n"); idx != -1 {
   376→			cleaned = cleaned[idx+1:]
   377→		}
   378→		// Remove closing fence.
   379→		if idx := strings.LastIndex(cleaned, "```"); idx != -1 {
   380→			cleaned = cleaned[:idx]
   381→		}
   382→		cleaned = strings.TrimSpace(cleaned)
   383→	}
   384→
   385→	var report models.SemanticReport
   386→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(cleaned), &report); err != nil {
   387→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("parse report JSON: %w", err)
   388→	}
   389→
   390→	if report.Summary == "" {
   391→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("report is missing required 'summary' field")
   392→	}
   393→
   394→	return &report, nil
   395→}
   396→
   397→// toolsToSlice is a helper to convert the tool.Tool interface slice for use
   398→// with llmagent.Config. This is kept for API compatibility in case the
   399→// function signature changes.
   400→func toolsToSlice(tools []tool.Tool) []tool.Tool {
   401→	return tools
   402→}

     1→package agent
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→
     7→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool"
     8→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool/functiontool"
     9→
    10→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/models"
    11→)
    12→
    13→// AgentDataStore defines the data access methods that agent tools use to
    14→// research releases and context sources for a given project.
    15→type AgentDataStore interface {
    16→	ListReleasesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.Release, int, error)
    17→	GetRelease(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Release, error)
    18→	ListContextSources(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.ContextSource, int, error)
    19→}
    20→
    21→// --- Tool input/output types ---
    22→
    23→// GetReleasesInput is the input for the get_releases tool.
    24→type GetReleasesInput struct {
    25→	Page    int `json:"page,omitempty"`
    26→	PerPage int `json:"per_page,omitempty"`
    27→}
    28→
    29→// ReleaseItem is a single release in the tool output.
    30→type ReleaseItem struct {
    31→	ID         string `json:"id"`
    32→	SourceID   string `json:"source_id"`
    33→	Version    string `json:"version"`
    34→	RawData    any    `json:"raw_data,omitempty"`
    35→	ReleasedAt string `json:"released_at,omitempty"`
    36→	CreatedAt  string `json:"created_at"`
    37→}
    38→
    39→// GetReleasesOutput is the output for the get_releases tool.
    40→type GetReleasesOutput struct {
    41→	Releases []ReleaseItem `json:"releases"`
    42→	Total    int           `json:"total"`
    43→	Page     int           `json:"page"`
    44→	PerPage  int           `json:"per_page"`
    45→}
    46→
    47→// GetReleaseDetailInput is the input for the get_release_detail tool.
    48→type GetReleaseDetailInput struct {
    49→	ReleaseID string `json:"release_id"`
    50→}
    51→
    52→// GetReleaseDetailOutput is the output for the get_release_detail tool.
    53→type GetReleaseDetailOutput struct {
    54→	ID         string `json:"id"`
    55→	SourceID   string `json:"source_id"`
    56→	Version    string `json:"version"`
    57→	RawData    any    `json:"raw_data,omitempty"`
    58→	ReleasedAt string `json:"released_at,omitempty"`
    59→	CreatedAt  string `json:"created_at"`
    60→}
    61→
    62→// ListContextSourcesInput is the input for the list_context_sources tool.
    63→type ListContextSourcesInput struct {
    64→	Page    int `json:"page,omitempty"`
    65→	PerPage int `json:"per_page,omitempty"`
    66→}
    67→
    68→// ContextSourceItem is a single context source in the tool output.
    69→type ContextSourceItem struct {
    70→	ID     string `json:"id"`
    71→	Type   string `json:"type"`
    72→	Name   string `json:"name"`
    73→	Config any    `json:"config"`
    74→}
    75→
    76→// ListContextSourcesOutput is the output for the list_context_sources tool.
    77→type ListContextSourcesOutput struct {
    78→	Sources []ContextSourceItem `json:"sources"`
    79→	Total   int                 `json:"total"`
    80→	Page    int                 `json:"page"`
    81→	PerPage int                 `json:"per_page"`
    82→}
    83→
    84→// toolFactory holds the store reference and project ID scoped to a single
    85→// agent run. Tools created from this factory are bound to a specific project.
    86→type toolFactory struct {
    87→	store     AgentDataStore
    88→	projectID string
    89→}
    90→
    91→// NewTools creates the ADK function tools for agent research, scoped to a
    92→// specific project. The returned tools use the provided store to query
    93→// releases and context sources belonging to projectID.
    94→func NewTools(store AgentDataStore, projectID string) ([]tool.Tool, error) {
    95→	f := &toolFactory{store: store, projectID: projectID}
    96→
    97→	getReleases, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
    98→		Name:        "get_releases",
    99→		Description: "Fetch recent releases for the current project. Returns a paginated list of releases with version, raw data, and timestamps. Use this to understand what new versions have been published.",
   100→	}, f.getReleases)
   101→	if err != nil {
   102→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create get_releases tool: %w", err)
   103→	}
   104→
   105→	getReleaseDetail, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
   106→		Name:        "get_release_detail",
   107→		Description: "Get detailed information for a specific release by its ID. Returns the full raw data payload from the upstream provider. Use this to inspect changelogs, commit lists, or other release metadata.",
   108→	}, f.getReleaseDetail)
   109→	if err != nil {
   110→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create get_release_detail tool: %w", err)
   111→	}
   112→
   113→	listContextSources, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
   114→		Name:        "list_context_sources",
   115→		Description: "List the context sources configured for this project. Context sources are references to runbooks, documentation, monitoring dashboards, and other background materials that help you produce richer analysis.",
   116→	}, f.listContextSources)
   117→	if err != nil {
   118→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create list_context_sources tool: %w", err)
   119→	}
   120→
   121→	return []tool.Tool{getReleases, getReleaseDetail, listContextSources}, nil
   122→}
   123→
   124→// getReleases is the handler for the get_releases tool.
   125→func (f *toolFactory) getReleases(ctx tool.Context, input GetReleasesInput) (GetReleasesOutput, error) {
   126→	page := input.Page
   127→	if page < 1 {
   128→		page = 1
   129→	}
   130→	perPage := input.PerPage
   131→	if perPage < 1 || perPage > 50 {
   132→		perPage = 20
   133→	}
   134→
   135→	releases, total, err := f.store.ListReleasesByProject(ctx, f.projectID, page, perPage)
   136→	if err != nil {
   137→		return GetReleasesOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("list releases: %w", err)
   138→	}
   139→
   140→	items := make([]ReleaseItem, 0, len(releases))
   141→	for _, r := range releases {
   142→		item := ReleaseItem{
   143→			ID:        r.ID,
   144→			SourceID:  r.SourceID,
   145→			Version:   r.Version,
   146→			RawData:   r.RawData,
   147→			CreatedAt: r.CreatedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z"),
   148→		}
   149→		if r.ReleasedAt != nil {
   150→			item.ReleasedAt = r.ReleasedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z")
   151→		}
   152→		items = append(items, item)
   153→	}
   154→
   155→	return GetReleasesOutput{
   156→		Releases: items,
   157→		Total:    total,
   158→		Page:     page,
   159→		PerPage:  perPage,
   160→	}, nil
   161→}
   162→
   163→// getReleaseDetail is the handler for the get_release_detail tool.
   164→func (f *toolFactory) getReleaseDetail(ctx tool.Context, input GetReleaseDetailInput) (GetReleaseDetailOutput, error) {
   165→	if input.ReleaseID == "" {
   166→		return GetReleaseDetailOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("release_id is required")
   167→	}
   168→
   169→	release, err := f.store.GetRelease(ctx, input.ReleaseID)
   170→	if err != nil {
   171→		return GetReleaseDetailOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("get release: %w", err)
   172→	}
   173→
   174→	out := GetReleaseDetailOutput{
   175→		ID:        release.ID,
   176→		SourceID:  release.SourceID,
   177→		Version:   release.Version,
   178→		RawData:   release.RawData,
   179→		CreatedAt: release.CreatedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z"),
   180→	}
   181→	if release.ReleasedAt != nil {
   182→		out.ReleasedAt = release.ReleasedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z")
   183→	}
   184→
   185→	return out, nil
   186→}
   187→
   188→// listContextSources is the handler for the list_context_sources tool.
   189→func (f *toolFactory) listContextSources(ctx tool.Context, input ListContextSourcesInput) (ListContextSourcesOutput, error) {
   190→	page := input.Page
   191→	if page < 1 {
   192→		page = 1
   193→	}
   194→	perPage := input.PerPage
   195→	if perPage < 1 || perPage > 50 {
   196→		perPage = 20
   197→	}
   198→
   199→	sources, total, err := f.store.ListContextSources(ctx, f.projectID, page, perPage)
   200→	if err != nil {
   201→		return ListContextSourcesOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("list context sources: %w", err)
   202→	}
   203→
   204→	items := make([]ContextSourceItem, 0, len(sources))
   205→	for _, s := range sources {
   206→		items = append(items, ContextSourceItem{
   207→			ID:     s.ID,
   208→			Type:   s.Type,
   209→			Name:   s.Name,
   210→			Config: s.Config,
   211→		})
   212→	}
   213→
   214→	return ListContextSourcesOutput{
   215→		Sources: items,
   216→		Total:   total,
   217→		Page:    page,
   218→		PerPage: perPage,
   219→	}, nil
   220→}
   221→

    12→// AgentWorker is a River worker that processes AgentJobArgs by running the
    13→// LLM agent orchestrator. When a job is dequeued, it loads the corresponding
    14→// agent run from the store and delegates to Orchestrator.RunAgent.
    15→type AgentWorker struct {
    16→	river.WorkerDefaults[queue.AgentJobArgs]
    17→	orchestrator *Orchestrator
    18→	store        OrchestratorStore
    19→}
    20→
    21→// NewAgentWorker creates a new AgentWorker backed by the given orchestrator
    22→// and store.
    23→func NewAgentWorker(orchestrator *Orchestrator, store OrchestratorStore) *AgentWorker {
    24→	return &AgentWorker{
    25→		orchestrator: orchestrator,
    26→		store:        store,
    27→	}
    28→}
    29→
    30→// Work processes a single AgentJobArgs job. It loads the agent run from the
    31→// database and runs the LLM agent through the orchestrator.
    32→func (w *AgentWorker) Work(ctx context.Context, job *river.Job[queue.AgentJobArgs]) error {
    33→	slog.Info("agent worker picked up job",
    34→		"job_id", job.ID,
    35→		"agent_run_id", job.Args.AgentRunID,
    36→		"attempt", job.Attempt,
    37→	)
    38→
    39→	run, err := w.store.GetAgentRun(ctx, job.Args.AgentRunID)
    40→	if err != nil {
    41→		slog.Error("agent worker failed to load agent run",
    42→			"job_id", job.ID,
    43→			"agent_run_id", job.Args.AgentRunID,
    44→			"err", err,
    45→		)
    46→		return fmt.Errorf("get agent run: %w", err)
    47→	}
    48→
    49→	slog.Info("agent worker starting run",
    50→		"job_id", job.ID,
    51→		"agent_run_id", run.ID,
    52→		"project_id", run.ProjectID,
    53→		"trigger", run.Trigger,
    54→	)
    55→
    56→	if err := w.orchestrator.RunAgent(ctx, run); err != nil {
    57→		slog.Error("agent worker run failed",
    58→			"job_id", job.ID,
    59→			"agent_run_id", run.ID,
    60→			"project_id", run.ProjectID,
    61→			"err", err,
    62→		)
    63→		return err
    64→	}
    65→
    66→	slog.Info("agent worker run completed",
    67→		"job_id", job.ID,
    68→		"agent_run_id", run.ID,
    69→		"project_id", run.ProjectID,
    70→	)
    71→	return nil
    72→}
    73→

     1→package routing
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"encoding/json"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"log/slog"
     8→	"net/http"
     9→	"time"
    10→
    11→	"github.com/riverqueue/river"
    12→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/models"
    13→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/queue"
    14→)
    15→
    16→// NotifyStore is the data access interface required by the notification worker.
    17→type NotifyStore interface {
    18→	GetRelease(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Release, error)
    19→	GetSource(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Source, error)
    20→	ListSourceSubscriptions(ctx context.Context, sourceID string) ([]models.Subscription, error)
    21→	GetChannel(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.NotificationChannel, error)
    22→	GetProject(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Project, error)
    23→	GetPreviousRelease(ctx context.Context, sourceID string, beforeVersion string) (*models.Release, error)
    24→	EnqueueAgentRun(ctx context.Context, projectID, trigger string) error
    25→}
    26→
    27→// NotifyWorker is a River worker that processes NotifyJobArgs.
    28→// It looks up the release, finds all source-level subscriptions, resolves
    29→// the notification channel for each, and dispatches via the appropriate sender.
    30→// After sending notifications, it checks the project's agent rules and
    31→// auto-triggers an agent run if the version criteria are met.
    32→type NotifyWorker struct {
    33→	river.WorkerDefaults[queue.NotifyJobArgs]
    34→	store   NotifyStore
    35→	senders map[string]Sender
    36→}
    37→
    38→// NewNotifyWorker creates a NotifyWorker with default senders for webhook, Slack, and Discord.
    39→func NewNotifyWorker(store NotifyStore) *NotifyWorker {
    40→	return &NotifyWorker{
    41→		store: store,
    42→		senders: map[string]Sender{
    43→			"webhook": &WebhookSender{Client: &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second}},
    44→			"slack":   &SlackSender{Client: &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second}},
    45→			"discord": &DiscordSender{Client: &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second}},
    46→		},
    47→	}
    48→}
    49→
    50→// Work processes a notify job: fetches the release, resolves subscriptions,
    51→// sends notifications to all matching channels, and checks agent rules to
    52→// auto-trigger agent runs when version criteria are met.
    53→func (w *NotifyWorker) Work(ctx context.Context, job *river.Job[queue.NotifyJobArgs]) error {
    54→	release, err := w.store.GetRelease(ctx, job.Args.ReleaseID)
    55→	if err != nil {
    56→		return fmt.Errorf("get release: %w", err)
    57→	}
    58→
    59→	subs, err := w.store.ListSourceSubscriptions(ctx, job.Args.SourceID)
    60→	if err != nil {
    61→		return fmt.Errorf("list subscriptions: %w", err)
    62→	}
    63→
    64→	for _, sub := range subs {
    65→		ch, err := w.store.GetChannel(ctx, sub.ChannelID)
    66→		if err != nil {
    67→			slog.Error("get channel failed", "channel_id", sub.ChannelID, "err", err)
    68→			continue
    69→		}
    70→
    71→		sender, ok := w.senders[ch.Type]
    72→		if !ok {
    73→			slog.Warn("unknown channel type", "type", ch.Type)
    74→			continue
    75→		}
    76→
    77→		msg := Notification{
    78→			Title:   fmt.Sprintf("New release: %s", release.Version),
    79→			Body:    string(release.RawData),
    80→			Version: release.Version,
    81→		}
    82→
    83→		if err := sender.Send(ctx, ch, msg); err != nil {
    84→			slog.Error("send notification failed", "channel", ch.Name, "err", err)
    85→		}
    86→	}
    87→
    88→	// Check agent rules and auto-trigger if criteria are met.
    89→	w.checkAgentRules(ctx, release, job.Args.SourceID)
    90→
    91→	return nil
    92→}
    93→
    94→// checkAgentRules evaluates the project's agent rules against the new release
    95→// and the previous release for this source. If any rule matches, an agent run
    96→// is enqueued. Errors are logged but do not fail the job — notification delivery
    97→// is the primary responsibility.
    98→func (w *NotifyWorker) checkAgentRules(ctx context.Context, release *models.Release, sourceID string) {
    99→	source, err := w.store.GetSource(ctx, sourceID)
   100→	if err != nil {
   101→		slog.Error("get source for agent rules", "source_id", sourceID, "err", err)
   102→		return
   103→	}
   104→
   105→	project, err := w.store.GetProject(ctx, source.ProjectID)
   106→	if err != nil {
   107→		slog.Error("get project for agent rules", "project_id", source.ProjectID, "err", err)
   108→		return
   109→	}
   110→
   111→	// Parse the project's agent_rules JSON.
   112→	var rules models.AgentRules
   113→	if len(project.AgentRules) > 0 {
   114→		if err := json.Unmarshal(project.AgentRules, &rules); err != nil {
   115→			slog.Error("unmarshal agent rules", "project_id", project.ID, "err", err)
   116→			return
   117→		}
   118→	}
   119→
   120→	// Determine the previous version for comparison.
   121→	var previousVersion string
   122→	prev, err := w.store.GetPreviousRelease(ctx, sourceID, release.Version)
   123→	if err != nil {
   124→		slog.Error("get previous release for agent rules", "source_id", sourceID, "err", err)
   125→		return
   126→	}
   127→	if prev != nil {
   128→		previousVersion = prev.Version
   129→	}
   130→
   131→	if CheckAgentRules(&rules, release.Version, previousVersion) {
   132→		trigger := fmt.Sprintf("auto:version:%s", release.Version)
   133→		if err := w.store.EnqueueAgentRun(ctx, source.ProjectID, trigger); err != nil {
   134→			slog.Error("enqueue agent run", "project_id", source.ProjectID, "err", err)
   135→		} else {
   136→			slog.Info("agent run triggered by version rules",
   137→				"project_id", source.ProjectID,
   138→				"version", release.Version,
   139→				"previous_version", previousVersion,
   140→			)
   141→		}
   142→	}
   143→}
   144→

   702→// EnqueueAgentRun creates an agent_run row and enqueues a River AgentJobArgs.
   703→// This follows the transactional outbox pattern for zero-loss guarantee.
   704→func (s *PgStore) EnqueueAgentRun(ctx context.Context, projectID, trigger string) error {
   705→	tx, err := s.pool.Begin(ctx)
   706→	if err != nil {
   707→		return fmt.Errorf("begin tx: %w", err)
   708→	}
   709→	defer tx.Rollback(ctx)
   710→
   711→	var runID string
   712→	err = tx.QueryRow(ctx,
   713→		`INSERT INTO agent_runs (project_id, trigger, status)
   714→		 VALUES ($1, $2, 'pending')

> AGENT

Good — configurable per-project via the `AgentPrompt`/`AgentRules`. Now let me propose design approaches.

## Design Approaches

### Current Problems
1. **Agent reads ALL releases** — no version scoping; wastes tokens and loses focus
2. **No web search** — agent can't verify binaries, check community sentiment, or find download links
3. **Report format is too generic** — SREs need actionable fields: subject line, risk level, download commands
4. **No multi-source waiting** — agent fires per-source, doesn't cross-reference

### Approach A: Version-Scoped Agent with Sub-Agent Architecture (Recommended)

**Prompt**: Add `{{VERSION}}` placeholder to `DefaultInstruction`. The orchestrator substitutes it from the trigger (`auto:version:v1.10.15`). The user message also includes the version.

**Google Search**: Use ADK-Go `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` via a **sub-agent** pattern (required by Gemini to mix function tools + grounding tools). Root agent delegates to a `search_agent` for web queries and a `data_agent` for DB tools.

**Report**: Expand `SemanticReport` struct with SRE-focused fields: `subject`, `risk_level`, `status_checks`, `changelog_summary`, `download_commands`, `download_links`.

**Multi-source waiting**: Add `wait_for_all_sources` boolean to `AgentRules`. The orchestrator checks if all project sources have a release matching the version. If not → set run status to `"waiting"`, re-enqueue with a delay. Max retries configurable.

### Approach B: Single Agent, Direct Google Search

Same as A but skip the sub-agent architecture — add `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` directly to the tools slice. Simpler but may hit the Gemini constraint of mixing grounding + function tools.

### Approach C: Custom Web Search Function Tool

Instead of Gemini-native Google Search, build a custom `web_search` function tool that calls a search API (SerpAPI, Google Custom Search). Works with any LLM provider (OpenAI too), but requires an additional API key.

**My recommendation is Approach A** because:
- Sub-agent pattern is the documented way to mix tool types in ADK-Go
- Gemini-native Google Search requires no extra API key
- Clean separation of concerns (data tools vs web research)
- The version-scoping and multi-source waiting are orthogonal and work with any approach

## Design: Version-Focused Agent with SRE Report + Multi-Source Waiting

### 1. Version-Scoped Prompt

The `DefaultInstruction` gets a `{{VERSION}}` placeholder. The orchestrator extracts the version from the trigger string (`auto:version:v1.10.15`) and substitutes it before building the agent.

**New prompt structure:**
```
You are a release intelligence agent analyzing version {{VERSION}} of a software project.

Focus ONLY on version {{VERSION}}. Cross-check this version across all available sources.

Use tools to:
1. Fetch releases and find the one matching {{VERSION}} from each source.
2. Inspect release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads) for {{VERSION}} only.
3. Review context sources for relevant background.
4. Use web search to verify binary/image availability, community sentiment, and known issues.

Your final response MUST be a JSON object with these fields:
{
  "subject": "🚀 Ready to Deploy: <Project> <Version> (<Risk Summary>)",
  "risk_level": "CRITICAL|HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW",
  "risk_reason": "Why this risk level (e.g., 'Hard Fork detected in Discord')",
  "status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available", ...],
  "changelog_summary": "One-line summary of key changes",
  "availability": "GA|RC|Beta",
  "adoption": "Percentage or recommendation (e.g., '12% of network updated')",
  "urgency": "Critical|High|Medium|Low",
  "recommendation": "Actionable 1-2 sentence recommendation for SRE team",
  "download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15", ...],
  "download_links": ["https://...", ...]
}
```

The user message also changes from generic "Analyze recent releases" to:
```
Analyze version {{VERSION}} for this project. Cross-check all sources and produce a semantic release report.
```

You are a release intelligence agent analyzing version {{VERSION}} of a software project.

Focus ONLY on version {{VERSION}}. Cross-check this version across all available sources.

1. Fetch releases and find the one matching {{VERSION}} from each source.
2. Inspect release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads) for {{VERSION}} only.
3. Check binary/image availability directly from the source data.
4. Review context sources for relevant background.
5. Use web search ONLY when you need additional context not available from sources
   (e.g., community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption stats, known issues).

}

### 2. Sub-Agent Architecture

The current single `release_analyst` agent becomes a **root agent** that delegates to two sub-agents:

```
root_agent (release_analyst)
  ├── data_agent     — has: get_releases, get_release_detail, list_context_sources
  └── search_agent   — has: geminitool.GoogleSearch{}
```

- **Root agent** receives the version-scoped instruction, orchestrates the workflow
- **Data agent** handles all DB queries (existing tools, scoped to project)
- **Search agent** handles web searches when the root agent needs external context
- ADK-Go's `agenttool.New()` wraps each sub-agent as a tool callable by the root

This is the documented pattern for mixing Gemini grounding tools with function tools.

### 3. Multi-Source Waiting

Added to `AgentRules`:
```go
type AgentRules struct {
    OnMajorRelease   bool   `json:"on_major_release,omitempty"`
    OnMinorRelease   bool   `json:"on_minor_release,omitempty"`
    OnSecurityPatch  bool   `json:"on_security_patch,omitempty"`
    VersionPattern   string `json:"version_pattern,omitempty"`
    WaitForAllSources bool  `json:"wait_for_all_sources,omitempty"` // NEW
}
```

**Flow when `wait_for_all_sources` is true:**

1. Agent job is picked up by `AgentWorker`
2. Before running the LLM, orchestrator checks: does every source in this project have a release matching the target version?
3. If **yes** → proceed with agent execution
4. If **no** → set run status to `"waiting"`, return a `river.JobSnooze(duration)` to re-enqueue after a configurable delay (e.g., 5 minutes)
5. Max retries before giving up (e.g., 12 attempts = ~1 hour)

### 4. Expanded SemanticReport Model

```go
type SemanticReport struct {
    Subject          string   `json:"subject"`
    RiskLevel        string   `json:"risk_level"`
    RiskReason       string   `json:"risk_reason"`
    StatusChecks     []string `json:"status_checks"`
    ChangelogSummary string   `json:"changelog_summary"`
    Availability     string   `json:"availability"`
    Adoption         string   `json:"adoption"`
    Urgency          string   `json:"urgency"`
    Recommendation   string   `json:"recommendation"`
    DownloadCommands []string `json:"download_commands,omitempty"`
    DownloadLinks    []string `json:"download_links,omitempty"`
    // Keep old field for backward compat during transition
    Summary          string   `json:"summary,omitempty"`
}
```

### 5. AgentJobArgs Changes

Add `Version` to the job args so the orchestrator knows which version to analyze:

```go
type AgentJobArgs struct {
    AgentRunID string `json:"agent_run_id"`
    ProjectID  string `json:"project_id"`
    Version    string `json:"version"` // NEW
}
```

The trigger already contains `auto:version:v1.10.15` but passing it explicitly is cleaner than parsing.

This is the documented ADK-Go pattern for mixing Gemini grounding tools with function tools.\n\n```\nroot_agent (release_analyst)\n  ├── data_agent     — has: get_releases, get_release_detail, list_context_sources\n  └── search_agent   — has: geminitool.GoogleSearch{}\n```\n\n- **Root agent**: Receives the version-scoped instruction, orchestrates the analysis workflow\n- **Data agent**: Handles all DB queries (existing function tools, scoped to project)\n- **Search agent**: Handles web searches when root agent needs external context\n- `agenttool.New()` wraps each sub-agent as a tool callable by the root\n\n**Imports added:**\n```go\nimport (\n    \"google.golang.org/adk/tool/agenttool\"\n    \"google.golang.org/adk/tool/geminitool\"\n)\n```\n\n**BuildAgent changes:**\n```go\n// Data sub-agent with function tools\ndataAgent := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{\n    Name:        \"data_agent\",\n    Description: \"Query project releases and context sources from the database.\",\n    Model:       llmModel,\n    Tools:       functionTools, // get_releases, get_release_detail, list_context_sources\n})\n\n// Search sub-agent with Google Search grounding\nsearchAgent := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{\n    Name:        \"search_agent\",\n    Description: \"Search the web for additional context about a release.\",\n    Model:       llmModel,\n    Tools:       []tool.Tool{geminitool.GoogleSearch{}},\n})\n\n// Root agent delegates to both\nrootAgent := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{\n    Name:        \"release_analyst\",\n    Description: \"Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.\",\n    Model:       llmModel,\n    Instruction: instruction, // version-scoped\n    Tools: []tool.Tool{\n        agenttool.New(\"data_agent\", dataAgent),\n        agenttool.New(\"search_agent\", searchAgent),\n    },\n})\n```\n\n### 3. Multi-Source Waiting\n\nConfigurable per-project via `AgentRules`. When enabled, the agent waits until ALL sources in the project have a release matching the target version before generating the report.\n\n**AgentRules addition:**\n```go\ntype AgentRules struct {\n    OnMajorRelease    bool   `json:\"on_major_release,omitempty\"`\n    OnMinorRelease    bool   `json:\"on_minor_release,omitempty\"`\n    OnSecurityPatch   bool   `json:\"on_security_patch,omitempty\"`\n    VersionPattern    string `json:\"version_pattern,omitempty\"`\n    WaitForAllSources bool   `json:\"wait_for_all_sources,omitempty\"` // NEW\n}\n```\n\n**Flow when `wait_for_all_sources` is true:**\n\n1. `AgentWorker` picks up job\n2. Max retries before giving up (e.g., 12 attempts = ~1 hour), then run with partial data\n\n**New store method needed:**\n```go\nListSourcesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string) ([]models.Source, error)\nHasReleaseForVersion(ctx context.Context, sourceID, version string) (bool, error)\n```\n\n**New agent_run status:** `\"waiting\"` (in addition to pending, running, completed, failed)\n\n### 4. Expanded SemanticReport Model\n\n```go\ntype SemanticReport struct {\n    // New SRE-focused fields\n    Subject          string   `json:\"subject\"`\n    RiskLevel        string   `json:\"risk_level\"`\n    RiskReason       string   `json:\"risk_reason\"`\n    StatusChecks     []string `json:\"status_checks\"`\n    ChangelogSummary string   `json:\"changelog_summary\"`\n    DownloadCommands []string `json:\"download_commands,omitempty\"`\n    DownloadLinks    []string `json:\"download_links,omitempty\"`\n\n    // Existing fields (kept for backward compat)\n    Summary        string `json:\"summary,omitempty\"`\n    Availability   string `json:\"availability\"`\n    Adoption       string `json:\"adoption\"`\n    Urgency        string `json:\"urgency\"`\n    Recommendation string `json:\"recommendation\"`\n}\n```\n\n### 5. AgentJobArgs Version Field\n\n```go\ntype AgentJobArgs struct {\n    AgentRunID string `json:\"agent_run_id\"`\n    ProjectID  string `json:\"project_id\"`\n    Version    string `json:\"version\"` // NEW — target version to analyze\n}\n```\n\nThe version is passed explicitly rather than parsed from the trigger string.\n\n**Propagation:** `EnqueueAgentRun` signature changes to accept version:\n```go\nEnqueueAgentRun(ctx context.Context, projectID, trigger, version string) error\n```\n\n### 6. AgentRun Model Change\n\nAdd `Version` field to `AgentRun` for persisting which version the run targets:\n```go\ntype AgentRun struct {\n    // ... existing fields ...\n    Version string `json:\"version,omitempty\"` // NEW — target version\n}\n```\n\n## Files Changed\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `internal/agent/orchestrator.go` | Version placeholder substitution in prompt, version-scoped user message, sub-agent architecture in `BuildAgent`, multi-source wait check before execution |\n| `internal/agent/tools.go` | No change to tool implementations (tools stay the same) |\n| `internal/agent/worker.go` | Pass version from job args, handle `\"waiting\"` status + `river.JobSnooze` |\n| `internal/models/semantic_release.go` | Expand `SemanticReport` struct with SRE fields |\n| `internal/models/project.go` | Add `WaitForAllSources` to `AgentRules` |\n| `internal/models/agent_run.go` | Add `Version` field |\n| `internal/queue/jobs.go` | Add `Version` to `AgentJobArgs` |\n| `internal/routing/worker.go` | Pass version to `EnqueueAgentRun` |\n| `internal/api/pgstore.go` | Update `EnqueueAgentRun` signature, add `HasReleaseForVersion` + `ListSourcesByProject` queries |\n| `internal/db/migrations.go` | Add `version` column to `agent_runs` table |\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- OpenAI provider web search (Gemini-only for now via `geminitool.GoogleSearch`)\n- UI changes for the new report format\n- Notification format changes (notification sends raw report text as before)\n"
}
```

    51→-- Source-level releases (detected from polling sources)
    52→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS releases (
    53→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    63→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS semantic_releases (

    73→);

    75→-- Join table: which source releases compose a semantic release
    76→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS semantic_release_sources (
    77→    semantic_release_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES semantic_releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    78→    release_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    79→    PRIMARY KEY (semantic_release_id, release_id)
    80→);
    81→
    82→-- Notification channels (standalone)
    83→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS notification_channels (
    84→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    85→    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    86→    type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    87→    config JSONB NOT NULL,
    88→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    89→    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
    90→);
    91→
    92→-- Subscriptions: two types (source-level and project-level)
    93→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS subscriptions (
    94→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    95→    channel_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES notification_channels(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    96→    type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL CHECK (type IN ('source', 'project')),
    97→    source_id UUID REFERENCES sources(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    98→    project_id UUID REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    99→    version_filter TEXT,
   100→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
   101→    CHECK (
   102→        (type = 'source'  AND source_id  IS NOT NULL AND project_id IS NULL) OR
   103→        (type = 'project' AND project_id IS NOT NULL AND source_id  IS NULL)
   104→    )
   105→);
   106→
   107→-- Agent runs (scoped to project)
   108→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS agent_runs (
   109→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
   110→    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
   111→    semantic_release_id UUID REFERENCES semantic_releases(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
   112→    trigger VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
   113→    status VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
   114→    prompt_used TEXT,
   115→    error TEXT,
   116→    started_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
   117→    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
   118→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
   119→);
   120→
   121→-- API authentication keys
   122→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS api_keys (
   123→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
   124→    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
   125→    key_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
   126→    key_prefix VARCHAR(12) NOT NULL,
   127→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
   128→    last_used_at TIMESTAMPTZ
   129→);
   130→
   131→-- Trigger for SSE: notify on new releases
   132→CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION notify_release_created() RETURNS trigger AS $$
   133→BEGIN
   134→    PERFORM pg_notify('release_events', json_build_object('type', 'release', 'id', NEW.id)::text);
   135→    RETURN NEW;
   136→END;
   137→$$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;
   138→
   139→DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS release_created_trigger ON releases;
   140→CREATE TRIGGER release_created_trigger
   141→    AFTER INSERT ON releases
   142→    FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION notify_release_created();
   143→
   144→-- Trigger for SSE: notify on semantic release completion
   145→CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION notify_semantic_release() RETURNS trigger AS $$
   146→BEGIN
   147→    IF NEW.status = 'completed' AND (OLD IS NULL OR OLD.status != 'completed') THEN
   148→        PERFORM pg_notify('release_events', json_build_object('type', 'semantic_release', 'id', NEW.id)::text);
   149→    END IF;
   150→    RETURN NEW;
   151→END;
   152→$$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;
   153→
   154→DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS semantic_release_trigger ON semantic_releases;
   155→CREATE TRIGGER semantic_release_trigger
   156→    AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE ON semantic_releases
   157→    FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION notify_semantic_release();
   158→`
   159→
   160→// RunMigrations applies River's schema and the application schema. Idempotent — safe to call on every startup.
   161→func RunMigrations(ctx context.Context, pool *pgxpool.Pool) error {
   162→	migrator, err := rivermigrate.New(riverpgxv5.New(pool), nil)
   163→	if err != nil {
   164→		return fmt.Errorf("create river migrator: %w", err)
   165→	}
   166→	if _, err := migrator.Migrate(ctx, rivermigrate.DirectionUp, nil); err != nil {
   167→		return fmt.Errorf("river migrations: %w", err)
   168→	}
   169→
   170→	if _, err := pool.Exec(ctx, schema); err != nil {
   171→		return fmt.Errorf("app migrations: %w", err)
   172→	}
   173→	return nil
   174→}

   127→// --- tests ---
   128→
   129→func TestSendProjectNotifications_WithSubscriptions(t *testing.T) {
   130→	projectID := "proj-1"
   131→	channelID := "ch-1"
   132→
   133→	store := &mockOrchestratorStore{
   134→		project: &models.Project{ID: projectID, Name: "My Project"},
   135→		projectSubscriptions: []models.Subscription{
   136→			{ID: "sub-1", ChannelID: channelID, Type: "project", ProjectID: &projectID},
   137→		},
   138→		channels: map[string]*models.NotificationChannel{
   139→			channelID: {
   140→				ID:     channelID,
   141→				Name:   "test-webhook",
   142→				Type:   "webhook",
   143→				Config: json.RawMessage(`{"url":"http://example.com/hook"}`),
   144→			},
   145→		},
   146→	}
   147→
   148→	orch := &Orchestrator{store: store, llmConfig: LLMConfig{GoogleAPIKey: "test-key"}}
   149→
   150→	run := &models.AgentRun{ID: "run-1", ProjectID: projectID}
   151→	result := &agentResult{
   152→		semanticReleaseID: "sr-1",
   153→		version:           "v2.0.0",
   154→		reportText:        "Major release with breaking changes.",
   155→		projectName:       "My Project",
   156→	}
   157→
   158→	// sendProjectNotifications calls defaultSenders() which creates real HTTP
   159→	// senders. To test without making HTTP calls, we test that the store
   160→	// methods are called correctly. The real HTTP send will fail (no server),
   161→	// but that is logged and not returned as an error.
   162→	//
   163→	// To properly unit test the send path, we verify that
   164→	// ListProjectSubscriptions and GetChannel are called by checking that the
   165→	// method does not panic and the store state is accessed.
   166→	orch.sendProjectNotifications(context.Background(), run, result)
   167→
   168→	// Verify ListProjectSubscriptions was called (implicitly — if it returned
   169→	// subscriptions, GetChannel must have been called for the channel).
   170→	// The send itself will fail because there's no HTTP server, but that's
   171→	// expected and logged. The key assertion is that the method completes
   172→	// without error and accesses the store correctly.
   173→}
   174→
   175→func TestSendProjectNotifications_NoSubscriptions(t *testing.T) {
   176→	projectID := "proj-1"

   178→	store := &mockOrchestratorStore{

   180→		projectSubscriptions: []models.Subscription{}, // no subscriptions
   181→		channels:             map[string]*models.NotificationChannel{},
   182→	}
   183→
   184→	orch := &Orchestrator{store: store, llmConfig: LLMConfig{GoogleAPIKey: "test-key"}}
   185→
   186→	run := &models.AgentRun{ID: "run-1", ProjectID: projectID}
   187→	result := &agentResult{

   190→		reportText:        "Initial release.",

   192→	}
   193→
   194→	// Should return immediately without attempting to send anything.
   195→	orch.sendProjectNotifications(context.Background(), run, result)
   196→}

     1→package agent
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→
     7→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
     8→	"google.golang.org/adk/model/gemini"
     9→	"google.golang.org/genai"
    10→
    11→	oaimodel "github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/agent/openai"
    12→)
    13→
    14→// LLMConfig holds the configuration for creating an LLM model.
    15→type LLMConfig struct {
    16→	Provider string // "gemini" or "openai"
    17→	Model    string // e.g. "gemini-2.5-flash", "gpt-5.2"
    18→
    19→	// Gemini
    20→	GoogleAPIKey string
    21→
    22→	// OpenAI
    23→	OpenAIAPIKey  string
    24→	OpenAIBaseURL string // defaults to https://api.openai.com/v1
    25→}
    26→
    27→// NewLLMModel creates a model.LLM based on the configured provider.
    28→func NewLLMModel(ctx context.Context, cfg LLMConfig) (model.LLM, error) {
    29→	switch cfg.Provider {
    30→	case "gemini", "":
    31→		if cfg.GoogleAPIKey == "" {
    32→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("GOOGLE_API_KEY is required for gemini provider")
    33→		}
    34→		return gemini.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, &genai.ClientConfig{

    44→	default:
    45→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unknown LLM provider: %q (supported: gemini, openai)", cfg.Provider)
    46→	}
    47→}

   236→
   237→```go
   238→func (s *PgStore) EnqueueAgentRun(ctx context.Context, projectID, trigger, version string) error {
   239→	tx, err := s.pool.Begin(ctx)
   240→	if err != nil {
   241→		return fmt.Errorf("begin tx: %w", err)
   242→	}
   243→	defer tx.Rollback(ctx)
   244→
   245→	var runID string
   246→	err = tx.QueryRow(ctx,
   247→		`INSERT INTO agent_runs (project_id, trigger, version, status)
   248→		 VALUES ($1, $2, $3, 'pending')
   249→		 RETURNING id`, projectID, trigger, version,
   250→	).Scan(&runID)
   251→	if err != nil {
   252→		return fmt.Errorf("insert agent run: %w", err)
   253→	}
   254→
   255→	if s.river != nil {
   256→		_, err = s.river.InsertTx(ctx, tx, queue.AgentJobArgs{
   257→			AgentRunID: runID,
   258→			ProjectID:  projectID,
   259→			Version:    version,
   260→		}, nil)
   261→		if err != nil {
   262→			return fmt.Errorf("enqueue agent job: %w", err)
   263→		}
   264→	}
   265→
   266→	return tx.Commit(ctx)
   267→}
   268→```

   474→
   475→**Files:**
   476→- Modify: `internal/agent/orchestrator.go`
   477→
   478→**Step 1: Write the test**
   479→
   480→Add to `internal/agent/orchestrator_test.go`:
   481→
   482→```go
   483→func TestBuildAgentReturnsAgent(t *testing.T) {
   484→	// BuildAgent requires a real LLM model which needs API keys.
   485→	// We test that the function signature accepts the version parameter
   486→	// and that it's callable. The actual LLM integration is tested via
   487→	// integration tests.
   488→	//
   489→	// This test verifies the function compiles with the new signature.

   491→		var store AgentDataStore
   492→		var project models.Project
   493→		var cfg LLMConfig

   495→	}

   498→
   499→**Step 2: Update BuildAgent imports**
   500→
   501→Add to the import block in `orchestrator.go`:
   502→
   503→```go
   504→"google.golang.org/adk/tool/agenttool"
   505→"google.golang.org/adk/tool/geminitool"
   506→```
   507→
   508→**Step 3: Rewrite `BuildAgent` with sub-agent architecture**
   509→
   510→```go
   511→func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig, version string) (agent.Agent, error) {
   512→	instruction := DefaultInstruction
   513→	if project.AgentPrompt != "" {
   514→		instruction = project.AgentPrompt + "\n\n" + instruction
   515→	}
   516→	instruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, "{{VERSION}}", version)
   517→
   518→	llmModel, err := NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig)
   519→	if err != nil {
   520→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create LLM model: %w", err)
   521→	}
   522→
   523→	// Create project-scoped function tools.
   524→	functionTools, err := NewTools(store, project.ID)
   525→	if err != nil {
   526→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create agent tools: %w", err)
   527→	}
   528→
   529→	// Data sub-agent: handles DB queries for releases and context sources.
   530→	dataAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   531→		Name:        "data_agent",
   532→		Description: "Query project releases and context sources from the database. Use this to fetch release lists, release details, and context sources like runbooks and documentation.",
   533→		Model:       llmModel,
   534→		Tools:       functionTools,
   535→	})
   536→	if err != nil {
   537→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create data sub-agent: %w", err)
   538→	}
   539→
   540→	// Build the root agent tools list.
   541→	rootTools := []tool.Tool{
   542→		agenttool.New("data_agent", dataAgent),
   543→	}
   544→
   545→	// Search sub-agent: Google Search grounding (Gemini only).
   546→	if llmConfig.Provider == "gemini" || llmConfig.Provider == "" {
   547→		searchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   548→			Name:        "search_agent",
   549→			Description: "Search the web for additional context about a release. Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources, such as community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption statistics, or known issues.",
   550→			Model:       llmModel,
   551→			Tools:       []tool.Tool{geminitool.GoogleSearch{}},
   552→		})
   553→		if err != nil {
   554→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("create search sub-agent: %w", err)
   555→		}
   556→		rootTools = append(rootTools, agenttool.New("search_agent", searchAgent))
   557→	}
   558→
   559→	// Root agent orchestrates data lookup and optional web search.
   560→	return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   561→		Name:        "release_analyst",
   562→		Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   563→		Model:       llmModel,
   564→		Instruction: instruction,
   565→		Tools:       rootTools,
   566→	})
   567→}
   568→```
   569→

   573→Expected: PASS (unused import `geminitool` and `agenttool` now used)
